# Ember & Ash — Decision Register

**Status:** tracking register, created in Run 0 (roadmap reconciliation), 10 November 2026. Development branch
`claude/kind-dijkstra-nqkxn1`; code evidence taken at `fa7b5b7` (A1, S1 and A2 integrated; tree identical to A2's head
`e045d67`). **Updated in Run 0.1** (canonical corrections and critical integrity, 10 November 2026) on branch
`claude/run0.1-integrity`: D13 Unique registry, the four D14 payment paths, D18 renown, the documented Chronicle/Lore
split, canonical v1.2 and the corrected observation numbering. **Updated in S2** ("The Living Smithy", 10 November
2026) on branch `claude/s2-living-smithy`: the working smith, the 1.5× pace, zero XP under the steward, clarity, early-game,
commerce and developer-tool work (canonical v1.3). **Updated in the Living Workshop** (11 November 2026) on branch
`claude/living-workshop`: B1 professions and per-worker competence, B2 workshop knowledge and Designs, C1 staged work
orders, several stations of a type, family at the workshop, the night pace and playtest observations #37–61 (canonical
v1.4).

**Authority.** [`EMBER_ASH_CANONICAL_VISION.md`](EMBER_ASH_CANONICAL_VISION.md) (Design Baseline 1.4) is the design authority.
This register is a tracking instrument derived from it. It does **not** change, extend or reprioritise the canonical
design. Where something is not decided by canonical v1.4, the entry says so (`UNDECIDED` or `PROPOSED`) and asks.

**Approval is not authorisation.** `APPROVED` means the scope is part of the approved design (or was explicitly authorised
technical work). No entry in this register authorises implementation; work starts only from an explicit user request
(CLAUDE.md §2.2). Older sources are historical evidence, never permission to override canonical (CLAUDE.md §2.6).

**Companion documents:** [`EMBER_ASH_ROADMAP_REGISTER.md`](EMBER_ASH_ROADMAP_REGISTER.md) (milestones, gates, dependencies,
risks) and [`EMBER_ASH_PLAYTEST_OBSERVATIONS.md`](EMBER_ASH_PLAYTEST_OBSERVATIONS.md) (playtest observations OBS-01 … OBS-61).

**Size:** 649 entries in this register, plus 61 playtest observations in the observation register.

---

## 0. How to read this register

### 0.1 Columns

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ID | Permanent identifier (see §0.6). |
| Requirement | What must be true, in one or two sentences. For `REJECTED` entries, the excluded thing. |
| Source | Most authoritative reference first (abbreviations in §0.5). |
| Scope | `APPROVED` · `PROPOSED` · `UNDECIDED` · `REJECTED` (§0.2). |
| Delivery | `NOT STARTED` · `PARTIAL` · `IMPLEMENTED` · `VERIFIED` · `BLOCKED` (§0.3). |
| Target | Milestone or `BACKLOG` (§0.4). |
| Dependencies · Evidence | Prerequisite entries or gates, code anchors (`file.gd:function`), tests (`t_case.test_name`), commits; `**See X**` / `**Related:**` mark cross-references (§1.7). |
| Open questions | What is still undecided for this entry, or `—`. |

### 0.2 Scope

| Value | Used when |
|---|---|
| `APPROVED` | Canonical v1.1 states it (LOCKED DESIGN, TARGET, decisions D1–D19, §14.1); **or** it is implemented pre-canonical baseline that canonical keeps (CV §2 "already implemented", §7.1 "foundations to extend", §12 "original implemented scope"); **or** it is technical work the user explicitly authorised in a milestone brief (A1, S1, A2, Run 0); **or** it is a defect in already-approved behaviour. |
| `PROPOSED` | Suggested by Claude (architecture-review TECHNICAL RECOMMENDATIONs, milestone and worker proposals) or raised by the user as an idea or observation, awaiting a decision. |
| `UNDECIDED` | Canonical lists it as an OPEN DETAIL (§15 "still to determine"); or an older brief asked for it, canonical is silent and it is not implemented; or sources contradict each other. |
| `REJECTED` | Canonical excludes it: §1.3 "what the game must not become", the last paragraph of §15 ("outside the approved design unless explicitly revisited") or a NOT APPROVED item, or it is superseded by a locked decision. The canonical label NOT APPROVED is recorded as `REJECTED` here (GOV-001); revisiting one needs an explicit decision and a canonical revision. |

Baseline features that canonical never names are `APPROVED` under the baseline rule but listed in §1.5 so the user can
confirm them in a future canonical revision.

### 0.3 Delivery

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NOT STARTED` | No code for it. For a `REJECTED` entry: the excluded thing does not exist (good). |
| `PARTIAL` | Some of it exists; the entry says what is missing. |
| `IMPLEMENTED` | Code present and reachable, but no specific automated test was found. For a `REJECTED` entry: the excluded thing **exists in code** (a conflict, §1.3). |
| `VERIFIED` | Implemented **and** exercised by a named automated test or rendered phase. Human Windows gameplay acceptance is tracked separately (REL-004) and is not implied. |
| `BLOCKED` | Cannot proceed: an unmet dependency or missing decision is named. |

### 0.4 Target

`BASELINE` delivered before the canonical lock (≤ build-0.14.6) · `A1` Protect the Foundation · `S1` Steward Mode · `A2`
Workshop Reliability & Early-Game Experience · `RUN0` this reconciliation · `RUN0.1` canonical corrections and critical
integrity · `S2` the Living Smithy · `LW` the Living Workshop (B1 + B2 + C1 and playtest
consolidation; entries of those gates keep their gate letter) · `A-REM` remaining Gate A readiness ·
`B1` worker capabilities and professions · `B2` player smithing specialisation and workshop Designs · `C1` staged
collaborative production · `D` Commissions v2 · `E` Equipment depth · `F` Negotiation, trade and procurement · `G` World
recognition · `H` Regional crisis proof · `I` Civilizations and authored stories · `J` Chronicle and campaign · `BACKLOG`
no canonical placement (placement needs a decision) · `—` not applicable (`REJECTED`).

Letters D–J follow canonical §12.2. B1, B2 and C1 are the Run 0 brief's sub-milestone names for canonical gates B and C
(see the roadmap register §2). `A-REM` is a tracking label for unfinished Gate A items, not a new milestone. A target names
where canonical places the work; it is **not** a schedule and not an authorisation.

### 0.5 Sources and abbreviations

| Abbreviation | Source |
|---|---|
| CV §n, CV Dn | `docs/EMBER_ASH_CANONICAL_VISION.md` v1.4 (identical text to `docs/Ember_and_Ash_Canonical_Vision_and_Roadmap_v1.4.docx`; checked word for word in the Living Workshop). Run 0 audited against v1.1; v1.2 changed wording only; v1.3 extended §4.5/D19 (the working smith, the pace); v1.4 added the Living Workshop paragraphs to §4.2–4.5, D19, §14.1 and §15; section and decision numbers are unchanged. |
| MD | `docs/Ember_and_Ash_Master_Design_2026-11-09.docx` (historical evidence). |
| AR | `docs/EMBER_ASH_ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_2026-11-09.md` (technical evidence; its §7 questions D1–D18 were later settled by CV D1–D18). |
| ST | `docs/EMBER_ASH_CURRENT_STATUS.md` (9 November 2026 status audit of build-0.14.6; now partly stale). |
| SMD | `docs/STEWARD_MODE_DESIGN.md`. |
| GD:file | `game/docs/<file>` (GAMEPLAY, PLAYGUIDE, TECHNICAL, TESTING, ITERATION_011_AUDIT, LIVING_REALM_STATUS, VERTICAL_SLICE_REPORT, DANSK). |
| B:name | A user brief from the project history (First Playable, It1, It002, Vertical Slice, Bases, Living Village, It009, It010, World Remembers, It012, UX, Living Realm, A1, S1, A2, Run 0). The briefs are not files in the repository; they are the user's instructions in the development sessions. |
| R:name, W:name | Claude's final report for a brief; a worker report inside a milestone (A1, S1, A2-econ, A2-exp). |
| OBS-nn | [`EMBER_ASH_PLAYTEST_OBSERVATIONS.md`](EMBER_ASH_PLAYTEST_OBSERVATIONS.md). |

### 0.6 IDs

IDs are permanent: never renumbered or reused. A superseded or duplicate entry is marked, not deleted. Prefixes:
`GOV` governance and exclusions · `TECH` technical foundations · `REL` release and acceptance · `WS` physical workshop ·
`STAFF` staff and professions · `PROD` production and Designs · `STW` Steward Mode · `EQ` equipment · `ECO` business and
payments · `ADV` adventurers · `WLD` town, reputation, militia · `CIV` civilizations and stories · `UX` user experience ·
`LORE` Lore and Chronicle · `BUG` known bugs and limitations · `PROP` proposals · `QST` design questions · `HA`/`HB`
requirements from pre-canonical briefs that canonical does not name · `OBS` playtest observations (separate register).

### 0.7 Method (Run 0)

Nine read-only audit passes — six by domain, two over the historical briefs, one over the playtest observations — each
read canonical v1.1 in full plus the Master Design, the architecture review, the status audit, the Steward design, the
`game/docs` notes, the historical briefs and reports, and **verified every delivery status against the code and tests** at
`fa7b5b7`. The lead cross-checked the outputs, confirmed key findings directly in code (for example `loot.gd` minting a
second Unique, `smithy.gd` renown from gold, `shop.gd` refunds and `adventurers.gd` spoils that do not conserve money),
aligned duplicates (§1.8), added entries for findings that had none (BUG-042 … BUG-050) and validated every row (ID
format and uniqueness, column count, vocabulary). Test evidence at `fa7b5b7`: compile 221 scripts, suite 283/283, unit
384/384; A2's 48-phase rendered regression and Windows CI (tests, export, install-and-launch smoke) passed on the
identical tree `e045d67`. Requirements that canonical already names were not duplicated from older briefs; the history
passes recorded only what canonical does not name.

### 1.3 Conflicts with canonical

**Rejected by canonical but present in code** (Scope `REJECTED`, Delivery other than `NOT STARTED`). One distinct
conflict remains, recorded twice: the separate Chronicle store (preserved and documented by the user's decision until
Gate J). Renown bought with 2,500 gold (GOV-030) was removed in Run 0.1; the smith who could master every craft
(STAFF-014, GOV-026, HA-027, HB-019) was retired in the Living Workshop (his other crafts stop at the basic level):

- **GOV-031** (implemented in code) — No separate Chronicle truth database; the Chronicle is a view of shared Lore.
- **LORE-017** (implemented in code) — A separate chronicle truth database (a history store competing with Lore).

**Other places where code contradicts canonical** (recorded as `APPROVED` requirements that the code does not yet meet):
- *Resolved in Run 0.1:* EQ-024 — loot could mint a second "Grimhold" Unique (D13); now one per campaign, old saves'
  pieces registered and kept whole (what legacy copies should show: QST-028). ECO-042, BUG-038 … BUG-041 — four payment
  paths did not conserve money (D14); now each moves money from one purse to another.
- *Resolved in the Living Workshop:* STAFF-006 — apprentices could only make what the smith had learned; specialists
  now own their craft (CV §4.2). Still open: STAFF-005, HA-025 — hirelings produce goods with no station (CV §1.2, §4.3).
- WLD-011, WLD-012 — one global reputation number that changes instantly everywhere (D10).
- ADV-012, LORE-015, WLD-018, WLD-019 — character sheets show a legacy destination instead of the live plan; paid
  rumours state invented facts; scouting claims an effect adventurers never feel (CV §1.2, §8.1, §11).
- EQ-022, EQ-053 — displayed affixes with no effect (CV §11 honest information; §6.2).
- BUG-042 — socketing a rune renames a piece "Runed …" and raises its rarity (CV §6.1 "preserve … names").
- ECO-045, BUG-046, BUG-047 — gifts, spare parts and pawnshop histories conjured rather than drawn from simulation
  state (CV §1.2, §10.3).
- EQ-015 / QST-022 — only pieces made at the Ironhand forge can become Epic; CV §6.2 states Epic without a maker
  restriction (open question rather than a firm conflict).
- TECH-023, CIV-007 — `game/docs/LIVING_REALM_STATUS.md` still frames civilizations around "allegiance" before the
  regional proof (D15, CV §10.2).

**A decision taken without explicit approval — resolved:** BUG-005 — during S1 the lead decided that the smith's fatigue
holds while the steward runs the forge. The user's S2 brief superseded it: the working smith now tires, rests and sleeps
(canonical v1.3; BUG-005 `REJECTED`).

### 1.8 Run 0 normalisations

Where two passes classified the same requirement differently, the lead aligned them (the entry's Open questions cell
records the change):

- **GOV-031** — deliv set to `IMPLEMENTED`: aligned with LORE-017: the rejected separate Chronicle store exists in code (conflict).
- **BUG-014** — scope set to `UNDECIDED`: aligned with ECO-043: whether D14 needs villager purses is an open question.
- **EQ-019** — scope set to `APPROVED`: implemented pre-canonical baseline kept under CV §2/§7.1 (as HB-050); confirmation requested.
- **UX-014** — scope set to `APPROVED`: implemented pre-canonical baseline kept under CV §2/§7.1 (as HB-006); confirmation requested.
- **WLD-020** — scope set to `APPROVED`: implemented pre-canonical baseline kept under CV §2/§7.1 (as HB-028); confirmation requested.
- **TECH-021** — scope set to `APPROVED`: the requirement is canonical (CV §8.1 'pursue agreed objectives'), as ADV-018.

### 1.9 Missing source material and ambiguities

1. **"Ember & Ash Master Roadmap Handover (20261109_Roadmap.txt)"**, cited in the canonical appendix as a design source,
   is not in the repository or the session history. The **eighteen whiteboard observations** and the design discussion
   that produced D1–D18 survive only as the Master Design's summary (MD §14).
2. **Observation lists.** "Observations #1–43 have already been implemented" (B:It010) and "Observation #80" (B:Living
   Realm) refer to a list that exists nowhere; #44–79 are unknown (HB-033, `BLOCKED`). The original wording of playtest
   observation 10 ("survival incentives") is not available.
3. **Run 0 observation numbering**: the brief labelled 11 items "11, 12, 13–14, 13, 14, 15 … 19". Corrected in Run 0.1:
   the observation register numbers them OBS-11 … OBS-21 in order and keeps the brief's labels for traceability.
4. **Never supplied:** the "Consolidated Godot 2D Master Foundation Prompt" (B:First Playable) and the visual-exploration
   reference images; the Living Realm brief's options A/B/C behind "Approved direction: B with elements of C"; Claude's
   report for the Equipment Display Bases brief.
5. **Canonical text points — clarified in canonical v1.2 (Run 0.1, editorial only):** §14 now counts D19 beside the
   eighteen audit decisions (QST-026); D15 names the Master Design's Phase 0–10 roadmap as restated in §12.1 and says the
   canonical document prevails over the Master Design's wording; the charter, §2 and the appendix date the 0.14.6
   baseline to adoption, point to these registers for later status and say where each design source is kept. CLAUDE.md
   now cites D1–D19 and lists the registers as evidence. No design decision changed.
6. **Two phase numberings.** The Living Realm brief's phases 1–10 (used in `game/docs/LIVING_REALM_STATUS.md`) differ
   from canonical Phases 0–10; the mapping is in the roadmap register §3.
7. **Sub-milestone mapping.** B1, B2 and C1 come from the Run 0 brief; canonical defines gates B and C only. Per-stage
   skill checks (canonical Gate B) are placed in B1. Duplicate stations (D6) and molds (D11) have no canonical gate
   (BACKLOG).
8. **Unverified claims:** progression of Steward Mode while minimised and after an OS sleep (STW-003; `Game._process` does
   not clamp `delta`); real-time stair walking by apprentices (WS-012); the override precedence of `user://overrides`
   versus same-name creator art (UX-010).

| GOV-002 | Canonical v1.1 is the single design authority; Master Design, older briefs, game/docs notes, AR and ST are evidence only and lose on disagreement. | CV charter, §16.1; CLAUDE.md §2, §2.6; D15 | APPROVED | IMPLEMENTED | A1 | Governance lock `88980b1` (A1 base), v1.1 `8904d36`; every A1/S1/A2 brief cites it | D15 says "This Master Design / original phase numbering govern" — "this" is ambiguous now that canonical supersedes the MD |
| GOV-003 | Every implementation session reads `docs/EMBER_ASH_CANONICAL_VISION.md` before planning or coding, again after compaction or a new session. | CLAUDE.md §2.1; B:Lock design (#796) | APPROVED | IMPLEMENTED | A1 | Stated in every worker report (W:A1, W:S1, W:A2-econ, W:A2-exp) | — |

## Part III — Known defects, proposals and open questions


### 15. Known bugs and limitations — `BUG` (61)
